for some
b_{1},b_{2}>0
. The proof can be in fact achieved by similar computations as in the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

7.
Linear theory for the inner problem

In this section, we develop a linear theory for the associated inner problem. Since the construction is independent of the spatial dimension
n
, we assume
n\geq 3
in this section unless specifically stated otherwise. Set

\mathcal{D}_{R}=\{(y,\tau)\ |\ y\in B_{R(\tau)},\ \tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)\},\quad{\partial}\mathcal{D}_{R}=\{(y,\tau)\ |\ y\in{\partial}B_{R(\tau)},\ \tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)\}.

We consider the associated linear problem

(7.1)

{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}\phi+f_{1}(y,\tau)\phi+f_{2}(y,\tau)y\cdot\nabla\phi+h(y,\tau)\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{R}

where

p=\frac{n+2}{n-2},\quad U(y)=(n(n-2))^{\frac{n-2}{4}}\left(1+|y|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}.

Throughout this section, we always assume that
f_{1},f_{2}
satisfy

(7.2)

f_{i}(y,\tau)=f_{i}(|y|,\tau)\mbox{ \ are radial in space},~i=1,2,\ \ |f_{1}|,|f_{2}|,|y||\nabla f_{2}|\leq C_{f}\tau^{-d},\ d>0,\ C_{f}\geq 0.

It is easier to make mode expansion by spherical harmonic functions when
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are radial. And it is very possible to generalize the linear theory without the assumption that
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are radially symmetric.

Recall that the linearized operator
\Delta+pU^{p-1}

has only one positive eigenvalue
\gamma_{0}>0
such that

(7.3)

\Delta Z_{0}+pU^{p-1}Z_{0}=\gamma_{0}Z_{0},

where the corresponding eigenfunction
Z_{0}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
is radially symmetric with the asymptotic behavior

Z_{0}(y)\sim|y|^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}e^{-\sqrt{\gamma_{0}}|y|}~\mbox{ as }~|y|\to\infty.

The bounded kernels of
\Delta+pU^{p-1}
are given by

Z_{i}(y)={\partial}_{y_{i}}U(y),\ i=1,2,\dots,n,\quad Z_{n+1}(y)=y\cdot\nabla U(y)+\frac{n-2}{2}U(y).

Define the weighted
L^{\infty}
norm

\|h\|_{v,a}:=\sup_{(y,\tau)\in\mathcal{D}_{R}}v^{-1}(\tau)\langle y\rangle^{a}|h(y,\tau)|

where
a\geq 0
is a constant. Throughout this section, we assume
R(\tau)
,
v(\tau)\in C^{1}(\tau_{0},\infty)
with the form

\displaystyle v(\tau)=a_{0}\tau^{a_{1}}(\ln\tau)^{a_{2}}(\ln\ln\tau)^{a_{3}}\cdots,\quad R(\tau)=b_{0}\tau^{b_{1}}(\ln\tau)^{b_{2}}(\ln\ln\tau)^{b_{3}}\cdots,\quad v(\tau)>0,\quad 1\ll R(\tau)\ll\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle v^{\prime}(\tau)=O(\tau^{-1}v(\tau)),\quad R^{\prime}(\tau)=O(\tau^{-1}R(\tau))

where
a_{0},b_{0}>0
,
a_{i},b_{i}\in{{\mathbb{R}}}
,
i=1,2,\dots
. For brevity, we write
v=v(\tau),~R=R(\tau)
.

We impose a linear constraint on the initial value
\phi(y,\tau_{0})
to handle the instability caused by
Z_{0}
. Consider the associated Cauchy problem

(7.4)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}(y)\phi+f_{1}(y,\tau)\phi+f_{2}(y,\tau)y\cdot\nabla\phi+h,&\mbox{ in }\mathcal{D}_{R},\\
\phi(y,\tau_{0})=e_{0}Z_{0}(y),&\mbox{ in }B_{R(\tau_{0})},\end{cases}

where
\tau_{0}
is sufficiently large.
Formally speaking, when
R\ll\tau^{\frac{d}{2}-\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
, we can expect that
f_{1}\phi+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi
is a small perturbation since
|f_{i}|\ll\tau^{-2\epsilon}R^{-2}\lesssim\tau^{-2\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-2}
in
\mathcal{D}_{R}
.

The construction of solution to (
7.4
) is achieved by decomposing the equation into different spherical harmonic modes. Consider an orthonormal basis
\{\Upsilon_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
made up of spherical harmonic functions in
L^{2}(S^{n-1})
,
namely eigenvalues of the problem

\Delta_{S^{n-1}}\Upsilon_{j}+\iota_{j}\Upsilon_{j}=0\mbox{ \ in \ }S^{n-1}.

where
0=\iota_{0}<\iota_{1}=\iota_{2}=\dots=\iota_{n}=n-1<\iota_{n+1}\leq\dots
and
\int_{S^{n-1}}\Upsilon_{i}(\theta)\Upsilon_{j}(\theta)d\theta=\delta_{ij}
.
More precisely,
\Upsilon_{0}(y)=a_{0},~\Upsilon_{i}(y)=a_{1}y_{i},~i=1,\cdots,n
for two constants
a_{0}
,
a_{1}
and the eigenvalue
\iota_{l}=l(n-2+l)
has
multiplicity

\begin{pmatrix}n+l-1\\
l\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}n+l-3\\
l-2\end{pmatrix}\mbox{ \ for \ }l\geq 2.

For
h(\cdot,\tau)\in L^{2}(B_{R(\tau)})
, we decompose
h
into the form

h(y,\tau)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}h_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}(y/r),\ r=|y|,\ h_{j}(r,\tau)=\int_{S^{n-1}}h(r\theta,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}(\theta)d\theta.

Write
h=h^{0}+h^{1}+h^{\perp}
with

h^{0}=h_{0}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{0},\ h^{1}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j},\ h^{\perp}=\sum\limits_{j=n+1}^{\infty}h_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}.

Also, we decompose
\phi=\phi^{0}+\phi^{1}+\phi^{\perp}
in a similar form. Then looking for a solution to problem (
7.4
) is equivalent to finding the pairs
(\phi^{0},h^{0}),~(\phi^{1},h^{1}),~(\phi^{\perp},h^{\perp})
in each mode.

The key linear theory for the inner problem is stated as follows.

Proposition 7.1
.

Consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}\phi+f_{1}\phi+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi+h(y,\tau)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}(\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)&\mbox{ in }\mathcal{D}_{R}\\
\phi(y,\tau_{0})=e_{0}Z_{0}(y)&\mbox{ in }B_{R(\tau_{0})}\end{cases}

where
n\geq 4
,
\|h\|_{v,2+a}<\infty
,
0<a<2
. Suppose that
R^{2}\ll\tau^{d-}
,
R_{0}=C\tau^{\delta}\gg 1
,
\delta\geq 0
and
R_{0}^{n+2}\ll\tau^{\min{\{1,d\}}-}
, then for
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large, there exists
(\phi,e_{0},c_{i})
solving above equation, and
(\phi,e_{0},c_{i})=({\mathcal{T}}_{3i}[h],{\mathcal{T}}_{3e}[h],c_{i}[h])
defines a linear mapping of
h
with the estimates

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi|+|\phi|\lesssim R_{0}^{n+1}v\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a},\quad|e_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})R_{0}^{2-a}(\tau_{0})\|h\|_{v,2+a},

\displaystyle c_{i}[h](\tau)=

\displaystyle-\big(\int_{B_{2}}\eta(y)Z_{i}^{2}(y)dy\big)^{-1}\left(\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(y,\tau)Z_{i}(y)dy+R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}O(v\|h_{i}\|_{v,2+a})\right),\quad i=1,\dots,n,

\displaystyle c_{n+1}[h](\tau)=

\displaystyle-\big(\int_{B_{2}}\eta(y)Z_{n+1}^{2}(y)dy\big)^{-1}\left(\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(y,\tau)Z_{n+1}(y)dy+R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}O(v\|h_{0}\|_{v,2+a})\right),

where
0<\epsilon_{0}<\frac{\min\{a,1\}}{2}
is a small constant,

h(y,\tau)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\Upsilon_{j}(\frac{y}{|y|})h_{j}(|y|,\tau),\quad h_{j}(|y|,\tau)=\int_{S^{n-1}}h(|y|\theta,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}(\theta)d\theta,

O(v\|h_{i}\|_{v,2+a})
linearly depends on
h_{i}
for
i=0,1,\dots,n
.

The proof of Proposition
7.1
is achieved by the following Proposition and by another gluing procedure (re-gluing).

Proposition 7.2
.

Consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}\phi+f_{1}\phi+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi+h(y,\tau)&\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R}\\
\phi(y,\tau_{0})=e_{0}Z_{0}(y)&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R}\end{cases}

where

\|h\|_{v,2+a}<\infty
,

a>0
and
h
satisfies the orthogonal condition

\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}h(y,\tau)Z_{i}(y)dy=0\mbox{ \ for all \ }\tau>\tau_{0},\quad i=1,\dots,n+1.

Assume
R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll\tau^{\min{\{1,d\}}}
.
Then for
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large, there exists a solution
(\phi,e_{0})=({\mathcal{T}}_{2i}[h],{\mathcal{T}}_{2e}[h])
which is a linear mapping of
h
with the estimates

\displaystyle\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi|+|\phi|\lesssim

\displaystyle v\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-n}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}

\displaystyle+v\left(\Theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}(|y|)\langle y\rangle^{-2}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\ln R\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}

\displaystyle+v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{n}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-1-n}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{1-n}\right)\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a}

\displaystyle+v\left(\Theta_{R,2+a}^{0}(|y|)+R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,2+a},

|e_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})\theta_{R(\tau_{0})\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\left(1+C_{f}\tau_{0}^{-d}\min\{\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln R(\tau_{0})\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}

where

(7.5)

\hat{a}_{0}=\begin{cases}a&\mbox{ \ if \ }a\neq n-2\\
(n-2)-&\mbox{ \ if \ }a=n-2\end{cases},\quad\hat{a}_{1}=\begin{cases}a&\mbox{ \ if \ }a\neq n-1\\
(n-1)-&\mbox{ \ if \ }a=n-1\end{cases},

(7.6)

\theta_{Ra}^{0}=\begin{cases}R^{2-a}&\mbox{ \ if \ }a<2\\
\ln R&\mbox{ \ if \ }a=2\\
1&\mbox{ \ if \ }a>2\end{cases},\quad\theta_{Ra}^{1}=\begin{cases}R^{1-a}&\mbox{ \ if \ }a<1\\
\ln R&\mbox{ \ if \ }a=1\\
1&\mbox{ \ if \ }a>1\end{cases},

(7.7)

\lambda_{R}=\begin{cases}R^{-2}&\mbox{ \ if \ }n=3\\
(R^{2}\ln R)^{-1}&\mbox{ \ if \ }n=4\\
R^{2-n}&\mbox{ \ if \ }n\geq 5\end{cases},\quad\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(r)=\begin{cases}R^{2-a}&\mbox{ \ if \ }a<2\\
\ln R&\mbox{ \ if \ }a=2\\
\langle r\rangle^{2-\min\{a,n-\}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }a>2\end{cases}.

Before we prove Proposition
7.1
, we first use Proposition
7.2
to prove Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Set
\phi(y,\tau)=\eta_{R_{0}}(y)\phi_{i}(y,\tau)+\phi_{o}(y,\tau)
, where
\eta_{R_{0}}(y)=\eta(\frac{y}{R_{0}})
. In order to find a solution
\phi
, it suffices to consider the following inner–outer gluing system for
(\phi_{i},\phi_{o})

(7.8)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{o}=\Delta\phi_{o}+J[\phi_{o},\phi_{i}]\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{R},\\
\phi_{o}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}{\mathcal{D}}_{R},\quad\phi_{o}=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})},\end{cases}

(7.9)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{i}=\Delta\phi_{i}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{i}+f_{1}\phi_{i}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{i}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{o}+h+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}(\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)&\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{2R_{0}},\\
\phi_{i}=e_{0}Z_{0}(y)&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{2R(\tau_{0})},\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle J[\phi_{o},\phi_{i}]=

\displaystyle f_{1}\phi_{o}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{o}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{o}(1-\eta_{R_{0}})+A[\phi_{i}]+h(1-\eta_{R_{0}}),

\displaystyle A[\phi_{i}]=

\displaystyle\Delta\eta_{R_{0}}\phi_{i}+2\nabla\eta_{R_{0}}\cdot\nabla\phi_{i}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\eta_{R_{0}}\phi_{i}-{\partial}_{\tau}\eta_{R_{0}}\phi_{i}.

Here
c_{i}(\tau)
is given by

c_{i}(\tau)=c_{i}[\phi_{o}](\tau)=C_{i}\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}(pU^{p-1}(z)\phi_{o}(z,\tau)+h(z,\tau))Z_{i}(z)dz,\quad C_{i}=-(\int_{B_{2}}\eta(y)Z_{i}^{2}(y)dy)^{-1}

such that the orthogonal conditions

\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}\left(pU^{p-1}(z)\phi_{o}(z,\tau)+h(z,\tau)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}(\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(z)\right)Z_{j}(z)dy=0\mbox{ \ for \ }j=1,\dots,n+1

are satisfied.

We reformulate (
7.8
) and (
7.9
) into the following form

(7.10)

\displaystyle\phi_{o}(y,\tau)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{o}[J[\phi_{o},\phi_{i}]],\quad\phi_{i}(y,\tau)={\mathcal{T}}_{2i}\left[pU^{p-1}(y)\phi_{o}+h+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}(\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)\right],

\displaystyle e_{0}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{2e}\left[pU^{p-1}(y)\phi_{o}+h+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}(\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)\right],

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{o}
is a linear mapping given by the standard parabolic theory, and
{\mathcal{T}}_{2i}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{2e}
are given by Proposition
7.2
.
We will solve the system (
7.10
) by the contraction mapping theorem.

Denote the leading term of the right hand side of (
7.9
) as
H_{1}:=h+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}C_{i}\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(z,\tau)Z_{i}(z)dz
. It is easy to check
\|H_{1}\|_{v,2+a}\lesssim\|h\|_{v,2+a}
. If
H_{1}
satisfies the orthogonal condition in
\mathcal{D}_{2R_{0}}
, under the assumption
R_{0}^{n+2}\ll\tau^{\min{\{1,d\}-}}
, Proposition
7.2
gives following a priori estimates

\langle y\rangle|\nabla{\mathcal{T}}_{2i}[H_{1}]|+|{\mathcal{T}}_{2i}[H_{1}]|\leq D_{i}w_{i}(y,\tau),\quad|{\mathcal{T}}_{2e}[H_{1}]|\leq D_{i}v(\tau_{0})R_{0}^{2-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a},

where
D_{i}\geq 1
is a constant and

w_{i}(y,\tau)=v\left(\lambda_{R_{0}}^{-1}R_{0}^{2-a}\langle y\rangle^{-n}+\theta_{R_{0}a}^{1}R_{0}^{n}\langle y\rangle^{-1-n}+\langle y\rangle^{-a}+R_{0}\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h\|_{v,2+a}

where
\theta_{R_{0}a}^{1}
is given in (
7.6
). For this reason, we will solve the inner part in the space

\mathcal{B}_{i}=\left\{g(y,\tau)\ :\ \langle y\rangle|\nabla_{y}g(y,\tau)|+|g(y,\tau)|\leq 2D_{i}w_{i}(y,\tau)\right\}.

For any
\tilde{\phi}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
, we will find a solution
\phi_{o}=\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]
of (
7.8
) by the contraction mapping theorem. Let us estimate
J[0,\tilde{\phi}_{i}]
term by term.
For
n\geq 4
,

|A[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]|\lesssim D_{i}v(R_{0}^{-2}+\tau^{-d})(R_{0}^{-a}\ln R_{0}+R_{0}^{-1})\mathbf{1}_{\{R_{0}\leq|y|\leq 2R_{0}\}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}\lesssim D_{i}vR_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\langle y\rangle^{-2-a_{1}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}

where constants
0<a_{1}<\min\{a,1\}
and
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{\min\{a,1\}-a_{1}}{2}
. Also we have

|h(1-\eta_{R_{0}})|\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{\{y\geq R_{0}\}}v\langle y\rangle^{-2-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a}\lesssim vR_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\langle y\rangle^{-2-a_{1}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Consider (
7.8
) with the right hand side
J[0,\tilde{\phi}_{i}]
. Using
Cv(-\Delta)^{-1}(\langle y\rangle^{-2-a_{1}})R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}
as the barrier function with a large constant
C
and then scaling argument, we have

\langle y\rangle|\nabla{\mathcal{T}}_{o}[J[0,\tilde{\phi}_{i}]](y,\tau)|+|{\mathcal{T}}_{o}[J[0,\tilde{\phi}_{i}]](y,\tau)|\leq w_{o}(y,\tau)=D_{o}D_{i}vR_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\langle y\rangle^{-a_{1}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}

with a large constant
D_{o}\geq 1
.
This suggests us solve
\phi_{o}
in the following space:

\mathcal{B}_{o}=\left\{f(y,\tau)\ :\ \langle y\rangle|\nabla f(y,\tau)|+|f(y,\tau)|\leq 2w_{o}(y,\tau)\right\}.

For any
\tilde{\phi}_{o}\in\mathcal{B}_{o}
, due to
|y|\leq 2R(\tau)
, we have

|pU^{p-1}\tilde{\phi}_{o}(1-\eta_{R_{0}})|\lesssim R_{0}^{-2}D_{o}D_{i}vR_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\langle y\rangle^{-2-a_{1}}\|h\|_{v,2+a},

|f_{1}\phi_{o}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{o}|\lesssim\tau^{-d}R^{2}(\tau)D_{o}D_{i}vR_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\langle y\rangle^{-2-a_{1}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Since
\tau^{-d}R^{2}
,
R_{0}^{-2}
provide smallness, by comparison principle, we have

{\mathcal{T}}_{o}[J[\tilde{\phi}_{o},\tilde{\phi}_{i}]]\in\mathcal{B}_{o}.

The contraction mapping property can be deduced in the same way.

Now we have found a solution
\phi_{o}=\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]\in\mathcal{B}_{o}
. It follows that

\Big\|pU^{p-1}(y)\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}C_{i}\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}pU^{p-1}(z)\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}](z,\tau)Z_{i}(z)dz\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)\Big\|_{v,2+a}\lesssim D_{o}D_{i}R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Due to the choice of
c_{i}(\tau)
,
H_{2}:=pU^{p-1}(y)\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]+h+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n+1}c_{i}[\phi_{o}[\tilde{\phi}_{i}]](\tau)\eta(y)Z_{i}(y)
satisfies the orthogonal condition in
{\mathcal{D}}_{2R_{0}}
.
By Proposition
7.2
, since
R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}
provides smallness, we have

{\mathcal{T}}_{2i}[h_{2}]\in\mathcal{B}_{i}

The contraction property can be deduced in the same way. Thus we find a solution

(7.11)

\phi_{i}=\phi_{i}[h]\in\mathcal{B}_{i}.

Finally we obtain a solution

(\phi_{o},\phi_{i})
for (
7.8
) and (
7.9
).

From the construction above and the topology of
\mathcal{B}_{i}
,
\phi_{i}[h]=0
if
h=0
, which deduces that
\phi_{i}[h]
is a linear mapping of
h
. By the similar argument,
\phi_{o}[h]
and
c_{i}[h]
are also linear mappings of
h
, and so does
\phi
.

We will regard
D_{o}
,
D_{i}
as general constants hereafter. Then by Propostition
7.2
and (
7.10
), we have

|e_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})R_{0}^{2-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Since
\phi_{o}[h]\in\mathcal{B}_{o}
, one has

c_{i}[h](\tau)=C_{i}\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(y,\tau)Z_{i}(y)dy+R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}O(v)\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Since the above operation is linear about
h
, we are able to decompose
h
into

h(y,\tau)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\Upsilon_{j}(\frac{y}{|y|})h_{j}(|y|,\tau),\quad h_{j}(|y|,\tau)=\int_{S^{n-1}}h(|y|\theta,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}(\theta)d\theta

and repeat the construction about
\Upsilon_{j}(\frac{y}{|y|})h_{j}(|y|,\tau)
separately. Then

c_{n+1}[h](\tau)=C_{n+1}\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(y,\tau)Z_{n+1}(y)dy+R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}O(v)\|h_{0}\|_{v,2+a},

c_{i}[h](\tau)=C_{i}\int_{B_{2R_{0}}}h(y,\tau)Z_{i}(y)dy+R_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}O(v)\|h_{i}\|_{v,2+a}\mbox{ \ for \ }i=1,\dots,n.

Reviewing the re-gluing procedure, we have

|J[0,\phi_{i}]|\lesssim R_{0}v\langle y\rangle^{-2-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Using comparison principle to (
7.8
) several times, the upper bound of
\phi_{o}
can be improved to

(7.12)

|\phi_{o}|\lesssim R_{0}v\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

Combining (
7.11
), (
7.12
) and then using scaling argument, we conclude

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi|+|\phi|\lesssim R_{0}^{n+1}v\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|h\|_{v,2+a}.

∎

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.2
. We first invoke a coercive estimate for the linearized operator

Lemma 7.3
.

[
5
, Lemma 7.2]

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for all sufficiently large
R
and all radially symmetric functions
\phi\in H_{0}^{1}(B_{R})

with
\int_{B_{R}}\phi Z_{0}=0
, we have

c_{0}\lambda_{R}\int_{B_{R}}|\phi|^{2}\leq Q(\phi,\phi),

where
\lambda_{R}
is given in (
7.7
) and

Q(\phi,\phi):=\int_{B_{R}}(|\nabla\phi|^{2}-pU^{p-1}|\phi|^{2})
.

Note that in
[
5
, Lemma 7.2]
, there is above coercive estimate only for higher dimensions
n\geq 5
. The proof in lower dimensions
n=3,~4
is in fact similar and by slight modifications.

Lemma 7.4
.

Consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}(1-\chi_{M})\phi+f_{1}\phi+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi+h\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{R}\\
\phi=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}{\mathcal{D}}_{R},\quad\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})}\end{cases}

where
\chi_{M}(y)=\eta(\frac{y}{M})
,
M>0
is a large constant,
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
,
\|h\|_{v,a}<\infty
,
a\geq 0
.
Then when
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large,
for
\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)
given in (
7.7
), the unique solution
\phi_{*}[h]
has the following estimate:

|\phi_{*}[h]|\lesssim C(M,a,n)v\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)\|h\|_{v,a}.

Proof.

Set
\bar{a}=\min\{a,n-\}
,
r=|y|
,
L_{M}\phi=\Delta\phi+pU^{p-1}(y)(1-\chi_{M})\phi
. Set a barrier function as
\bar{\phi}(r,\tau)=Cvg(r,R)
, where

L_{M}g(r,R)=-\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}},\quad g(r,R)=g_{2}(r)\int_{r}^{R}\frac{d\rho}{g_{2}^{2}(\rho)\rho^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{\rho}g_{2}(s)s^{n-1}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}ds

and
g_{2}(r)>0
is the positive kernel of
L_{M}
and
g_{2}(r)\sim 1
for
r\in(0,\infty)
. By direct calculation, one has

\displaystyle\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}g(r,R)\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}\Theta_{R\bar{a}}^{0}(|y|)\lesssim R^{2}\ln R.

By scaling argument, one has
\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}|r{\partial}_{r}g(r,R)|\lesssim R^{2}\ln R
. Then

\displaystyle P(\bar{\phi}):=L_{M}(Cvg(r,R))+h(y,\tau)+Cv(f_{1}g(r,R)+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}g(r,R))-{\partial}_{\tau}(Cvg(r,R))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}+h(y,\tau)+Cv(f_{1}g(r,R)+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}g(r,R))-Cv^{\prime}g(r,R)-\frac{Cvg_{2}(r)R^{\prime}}{g_{2}^{2}(R)R^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{R}g_{2}(s)s^{n-1}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}\Bigg[-1+\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}(f_{1}g(r,R)+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}g(r,R))-v^{\prime}v^{-1}g(r,R)\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}g_{2}(r)R^{\prime}}{g_{2}^{2}(R)R^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{R}g_{2}(s)s^{n-1}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}ds\Bigg]+v\langle r\rangle^{-a}\|h\|_{v,a}\leq-\frac{3}{4}Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}+v\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}\|h\|_{v,a}

where we have used

|\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}(f_{1}g(r,R)+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}g(r,R))|\lesssim(|f_{1}|+|f_{2}|)R^{2}\ln R\lesssim C_{f}\tau^{-d}R^{2}\ln R\ll 1,

|v^{\prime}v^{-1}g(r,R)\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}|\lesssim|v^{\prime}|v^{-1}R^{2}\ln R\lesssim\tau^{-1}R^{2}\ln R\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}g_{2}(r)|R^{\prime}|}{g_{2}^{2}(R)R^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{R}g_{2}(s)s^{n-1}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}ds\sim\frac{\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}|R^{\prime}|}{R^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{R}s^{n-1}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}ds\lesssim R|R^{\prime}|\lesssim\tau^{-1}R^{2}\ll 1.

Set
C=2\|h\|_{v,a}
, then
P(\bar{\phi})\leq 0
.

∎

7.1.
Mode
0
without orthogonality

Lemma 7.5
.

Consider

(7.13)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi^{0}=\Delta\phi^{0}+pU^{p-1}\phi^{0}+f_{1}\phi^{0}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi^{0}+h^{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R},\\
\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})=e_{0}Z_{0}(y)&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})}\end{cases}

where
\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}<\infty
,
a\geq 0
. Assume
\lambda_{R}\tau^{d}\gg 1
,
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
.
Then for
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large, there exists a linear mapping
(\phi^{0},e_{0})=({\mathcal{T}}_{1i}[h^{0}],{\mathcal{T}}_{1e}[h^{0}])
solving (
7.13
) with the following estimates

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi^{0}|+|\phi^{0}|\lesssim v\left(\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{Ra}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{2-n}+\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,a},

|e_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})\theta_{R(\tau_{0})a}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}.

Proof.

First, we decompose
\phi^{0}
into two parts

\phi^{0}=\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+\tilde{\phi},

where
\phi_{*}[h^{0}]
is the solution derived from Lemma
7.4
with the following estimate

(7.14)

|\phi_{*}[h^{0}]|\lesssim v\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}.

Then

\displaystyle{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+{\partial}_{\tau}\tilde{\phi}=

\displaystyle\Delta\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+\Delta\tilde{\phi}+pU^{p-1}(y)\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+pU^{p-1}(y)(1-\chi_{M})\phi_{*}[h^{0}]

\displaystyle+pU^{p-1}(y)\tilde{\phi}+f_{1}(\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+\tilde{\phi})+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla(\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+\tilde{\phi})+h^{0}\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R},

which implies that

{\partial}_{\tau}\tilde{\phi}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}+pU^{p-1}(y)\tilde{\phi}+f_{1}\tilde{\phi}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}+pU^{p-1}(y)\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R}.

We will construct a linear mapping
\tilde{\phi}=\tilde{\phi}[h^{0}]
.
Take
\tilde{\phi}=\tilde{\phi}_{1}+e(\tau)Z_{0}(y)

and
consider the following equation

(7.15)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\tilde{\phi}_{1}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}_{1}+pU^{p-1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+f_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}-{\partial}_{\tau}e(\tau)Z_{0}+\gamma_{0}e(\tau)Z_{0}\\
\qquad\qquad+pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]+e(\tau)(f_{1}Z_{0}(y)+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla Z_{0}(y))\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{R},\\
\tilde{\phi}_{1}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}{\mathcal{D}}_{R},\quad\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})},\quad\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,\tau)Z_{0}(y)dy=0\quad\forall\tau>\tau_{0}.\end{cases}

Here

e(\tau)
will be chosen to make
\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,\tau)Z_{0}(y)dy=0
for all
\tau>\tau_{0}
. Indeed, multiplying (
7.15
) by
Z_{0}
and integrating by parts, one has

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\tilde{\phi}_{1}Z_{0}(y)dy=\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}{\partial}_{\tau}\tilde{\phi}_{1}Z_{0}(y)dy=\gamma_{0}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\tilde{\phi}_{1}Z_{0}(y)dy+\int_{{\partial}B_{R(\tau)}}Z_{0}(y){\partial}_{n}\tilde{\phi}_{1}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1})Z_{0}(y)dy-({\partial}_{\tau}e(\tau)-\gamma_{0}e(\tau))\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}Z_{0}^{2}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]Z_{0}(y)dy+e(\tau)\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}Z_{0}(y)+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla Z_{0}(y))Z_{0}(y)dy.

By
\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0
, the orthogonality
\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,\tau)Z_{0}(y)dy=0
holds for all
\tau>\tau_{0}
if and only if

\displaystyle{\partial}_{\tau}e(\tau)-\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(\tau)e(\tau)=

\displaystyle\big(\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}Z_{0}^{2}(y)dy\big)^{-1}\Bigg[\int_{{\partial}B_{R(\tau)}}Z_{0}(y){\partial}_{n}\tilde{\phi}_{1}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1})Z_{0}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]Z_{0}(y)dy\Bigg],

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(\tau)=\gamma_{0}+(\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}Z_{0}^{2}(y)dy)^{-1}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}Z_{0}(y)+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla Z_{0}(y))Z_{0}(y)dy
. By (
7.2
),
\lim\limits_{\tau\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(\tau)=\gamma_{0}
as
\tau\rightarrow\infty
. We take
e(\tau)
as

\displaystyle e(\tau)=-e^{\int^{\tau}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\int^{s}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\big(\int_{B_{R(s)}}Z_{0}^{2}(y)dy\big)^{-1}\Bigg[\int_{{\partial}B_{R(s)}}Z_{0}(y){\partial}_{n}\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,s)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R(s)}}(f_{1}(y,s)\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,s)+f_{2}(y,s)y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y,s))Z_{0}(y)dy+\int_{B_{R(s)}}pU^{p-1}(y)\chi_{M}(y)\phi_{*}[h^{0}](y,s)Z_{0}(y)dy\Bigg]ds.

Set

\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}=\sup\limits_{\tau>\tau_{0}}\left(\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\right)^{-1}\left(\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(\tau)})}+\|\langle\cdot\rangle\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(\tau)})}\right).

By (
7.14
), it is straightforward to get

(7.16)

\displaystyle|e(\tau)|\lesssim e^{\int^{\tau}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\int^{s}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\Big(e^{-cR(s)}\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(s)})}+\||f_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}|+|f_{2}\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}|\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(s)})}+v(s)\theta_{Ra}^{0}(s)\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}\Big)ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle e^{\int^{\tau}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\int^{s}\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(u)du}\Big[(s^{-d}+e^{-cR(s)})\min\{s^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s)\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s)v(s)\theta_{Ra}^{0}(s)\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}+v(s)\theta_{Ra}^{0}(s)\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}+v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}

for some constant
c>0
, and
\theta_{Ra}^{0}
is given in (
7.6
). It follows that

|{\partial}_{\tau}e(\tau)|\lesssim(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}+v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}.

With the above choice of
e(\tau)
, the global existence of (
7.15
) can be deduced by the local existence.

Multiplying equation (
7.15
) by
\tilde{\phi}_{1}
and integrating by parts, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}|^{2}-pU^{p-1}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2})dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1})\tilde{\phi}_{1}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}]\tilde{\phi}_{1}dy+e(\tau)\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(f_{1}Z_{0}(y)+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla Z_{0}(y))\tilde{\phi}_{1}dy.

Then by Lemma
7.3
and (
7.2
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy+c\lambda_{R}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy\leq C\tau^{-d}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\frac{4}{c\lambda_{R}}(pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{0}])^{2}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\frac{c\lambda_{R}}{4}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\frac{4}{c\lambda_{R}}e^{2}(\tau)(f_{1}Z_{0}(y)+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla Z_{0}(y))^{2}dy+\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}\frac{c\lambda_{R}}{4}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy

for some constant
c>0
. By (
7.2
), (
7.14
), (
7.16
) and the assumption
\lambda_{R}\tau^{d}\gg 1
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy+\frac{c\lambda_{R}}{4}\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy\lesssim

\displaystyle\lambda_{R}^{-1}[(v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a})^{2}+\tau^{-2d}e^{2}(\tau)]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lambda_{R}^{-1}(v\theta_{Ra}^{0})^{2}\left[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}\right]^{2}.

Since
\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R(\tau)}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{2}dy\lesssim

\displaystyle e^{-\int^{\tau}\frac{c\lambda_{R}(u)}{2}du}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}e^{\int^{s}\frac{c\lambda_{R}(u)}{2}du}\lambda_{R}^{-1}(s)(v(s)\theta_{Ra}^{0}(s))^{2}[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}]^{2}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\tau,\lambda_{R}^{-1}\}\lambda_{R}^{-1}(v\theta_{Ra}^{0})^{2}\left[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}\right]^{2}.

Applying parabolic estimate to (
7.15
), one has

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(\tau)})}\lesssim\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}]

\displaystyle+\tau^{-d}(\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(\tau)})}+\|y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R(\tau)})})+|{\partial}_{\tau}e(\tau)|+|e(\tau)|+v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}].

By comparison principle, the spatial decay of
\tilde{\phi}_{1}
can be improved and scaling argument will give the spatial decay about
\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}
. Then one has

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}|+|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|\lesssim\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}[\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}+(\tau_{0}^{-d}+e^{-cR(\tau_{0})})\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{w}]\langle y\rangle^{2-n},

which implies

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}|+|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|\lesssim\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}.

Reviewing the computations in (
7.16
) and using
\lambda_{R}\tau^{d}\gg 1
, one has
|e(\tau)|\lesssim v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}
and
then

(7.17)

|\tilde{\phi}|=|\tilde{\phi}_{1}+e(\tau)Z_{0}(y)|\lesssim\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{Ra}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\|h^{0}\|_{v,a}

Finally, we take
e_{0}=e(\tau_{0})
.
Combining (
7.14
) and (
7.17
), we complete the proof of this Lemma.
∎

7.2.
Modes
1
to
n
without orthogonality

Lemma 7.6
.

Consider

(7.18)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi^{1}=\Delta\phi^{1}+pU^{p-1}\phi^{1}+f_{1}\phi^{1}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi^{1}+h^{1}(y,\tau)\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R}\\
\phi^{1}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathcal{D}_{R}\quad\phi^{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})}\end{cases}

where

h^{1}(y,\tau)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}(|y|,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}
.
Assume
R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll\tau^{\min{\{1,d\}}}
,
where
\theta_{Ra}^{1}
is given in (
7.6
). Then for
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large, there exists a unique linear mapping
\phi^{1}=\phi^{1}[h^{1}]
solving (
7.18
) of the form

\phi^{1}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(|y|,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}

with the following estimate

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi^{1}|+|\phi^{1}|\lesssim v\theta_{Ra}^{1}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{1-n}\|h^{1}\|_{v,a}.

Proof.

Set
r=|y|
. Notice
y\cdot\nabla(\phi_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j})=r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}
. It is equivalent to considering

(7.19)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{j}={\mathcal{L}}_{1}[\phi_{j}]+f_{1}\phi_{j}(r,\tau)+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}(r,\tau)+h_{j}(r,\tau)\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau)),\ \tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}(0,\tau)=0=\phi_{j}(R(\tau),\tau)\mbox{ \ for \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty),\quad\phi_{j}(r,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau_{0}))\end{cases}

where

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}[\phi_{j}]:={\partial}_{rr}\phi_{j}+\frac{n-1}{r}{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}-\frac{n-1}{r^{2}}\phi_{j}+pU(r)^{p-1}\phi_{j}
,
|h_{j}|\leq v\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|h_{j}\|_{v,a}
,
\|h_{j}\|_{v,a}\lesssim\|h^{1}\|_{v,a}
.

One positive kernel of
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
is given by

Z(r):=-U_{r}=(n(n-2))^{\frac{n-2}{4}}(n-2)r(1+r^{2})^{-\frac{n}{2}}
. Set a barrier function of (
7.19
) as
\phi_{s}=Cv\bar{\phi}(r,R)
, where

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}[\bar{\phi}]=-\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}},\quad\bar{a}=\min\{a,n-1\}

with

\bar{\phi}
given by the variation of parameter formula

\bar{\phi}(r,R)=Z(r)\int_{r}^{R}\frac{1}{\rho^{n-1}Z^{2}(\rho)}\int_{0}^{\rho}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}Z(s)s^{n-1}dsd\rho.

Then

\bar{\phi}\lesssim R^{n}\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}r\langle r\rangle^{-n},\quad|{\partial}_{R}\bar{\phi}|=\left|\frac{Z(r)}{R^{n-1}Z^{2}(R)}\int_{0}^{R}\langle s\rangle^{-\bar{a}}Z(s)s^{n-1}ds\right|\lesssim R^{n-1}\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}r\langle r\rangle^{-n}

for all
r>0
. This estimate holds for all
n>2
, and
a\leq 0
is also allowed here.
Next, we compute

\displaystyle P(\phi_{s}):={\mathcal{L}}_{1}\phi_{s}+f_{1}\phi_{s}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{s}-{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{s}+h_{j}=-Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}+Cv(f_{1}\bar{\phi}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\bar{\phi})-Cv^{\prime}\bar{\phi}-Cv{\partial}_{R}\bar{\phi}R^{\prime}+h_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}\left[-1+\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}(f_{1}\bar{\phi}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\bar{\phi})-v^{-1}v^{\prime}\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}\bar{\phi}-\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}{\partial}_{R}\bar{\phi}R^{\prime}+C^{-1}\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}-a}\|h_{j}\|_{v,a}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cv\langle r\rangle^{-\bar{a}}(-\frac{3}{4}+C^{-1}\|h_{j}\|_{v,a})

where we have used

|\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}(f_{1}\bar{\phi}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\bar{\phi})|\lesssim\tau^{-d}R^{n}\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}+1-n}\lesssim\tau^{-d}R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll 1,

|v^{-1}v^{\prime}\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}\bar{\phi}|\lesssim\tau^{-1}R^{n}\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}+1-n}\lesssim\tau^{-1}R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll 1,

|\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}{\partial}_{R}\bar{\phi}R^{\prime}|\lesssim\langle r\rangle^{\bar{a}}R^{n-1}\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}r\langle r\rangle^{-n}|R^{\prime}|\lesssim\tau^{-1}R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll 1

by (
7.2
),
\theta_{R\bar{a}}^{1}=\theta_{Ra}^{1}
and the assumption
R^{n}\theta_{Ra}^{1}\ll\tau^{\min{\{1,d\}}}
. Taking
C=4\|h_{j}\|_{v,a}
, one has
P(\phi_{s})<0
.

∎

7.3.
Higher modes

Lemma 7.7
.

Consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi^{\perp}=\Delta\phi^{\perp}+pU^{p-1}\phi^{\perp}+f_{1}\phi^{\perp}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi^{\perp}+h^{\perp}\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R}\\
\phi^{\perp}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathcal{D}_{R},\quad\phi^{\perp}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})}\end{cases}

where
\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}<\infty
,
a\geq 0
. Assume
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
.
Then there exists a unique linear mapping
\phi^{\perp}=\phi^{\perp}[h^{\perp}]
of the form

(7.20)

\phi^{\perp}=\sum\limits_{j=n+1}^{\infty}\phi_{j}^{\perp}(|y|,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}

with the following estimate

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi^{\perp}|+|\phi^{\perp}|\lesssim v\left(\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)+\theta_{Ra}^{0}R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}.

First we give the following technical lemma.

Lemma 7.8
.

For
f\in C^{2}(B_{R})\cap C_{0}(B_{R})
, by the expansion of spherical harmonic functions,

f=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}f_{j}(r)\Upsilon_{j},

where
r=|y|
,
f_{j}(r)=\int_{S^{n-1}}f(r\theta)\Upsilon_{j}(\theta)d\theta\in C^{2}[0,R]
. Then

Q(f,f)=\int_{B_{R}}(|\nabla f|^{2}-pU^{p-1}f^{2})dy=|S^{n-1}|\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}Q_{j}(f_{j},f_{j}),

where
|S^{n-1}|
is the volume of the unit
(n-1)
-sphere and

Q_{j}(f_{j},f_{j})=\int_{0}^{R}\left(f_{j}^{\prime 2}+\frac{\iota_{j}}{r^{2}}f_{j}^{2}-pU^{p-1}f_{j}^{2}\right)r^{n-1}dr.

Specially, if
f_{j}=0
for
j=0,1,\dots,n
, it holds that

(7.21)

\displaystyle Q(f,f)\geq(n+1)\int_{B_{R}}\frac{|f|^{2}}{|y|^{2}}dy.

Proof.

Since
\Delta_{S^{n-1}}\Upsilon_{i}=-\iota_{i}\Upsilon_{i}
,
\iota_{i}=i(n-2+i)
for a nonnegative integer
i
, we have

\Delta(f_{i}\Upsilon_{i})=(f_{i}^{\prime\prime}+\frac{n-1}{r}f_{i}^{\prime}-\frac{\iota_{i}}{r^{2}}f_{i})\Upsilon_{i}.

f|_{{\partial}B_{R}}=0
implies

f_{j}(R)=0
,
j=0,1,\dots
. Then

\displaystyle Q(f,f)=\int_{B_{R}}|\nabla f|^{2}-pU^{p-1}f^{2}dy=-\int_{B_{R}}(f\Delta f+pU^{p-1}f^{2})dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-|S^{n-1}|\int_{0}^{R}\left[\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}f_{i}(f_{i}^{\prime\prime}+\frac{n-1}{r}f_{i}^{\prime}-\frac{\iota_{i}}{r^{2}}f_{i})+pU^{p-1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}f_{i}^{2}\right]r^{n-1}dr=|S^{n-1}|\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}Q_{i}(f_{i},f_{i}).

For
i\geq n+1
,
\iota_{i}\geq 2n
, we have

Q_{i}(f_{i},f_{i})\geq Q_{1}(f_{i},f_{i})+(n+1)\int_{0}^{R}\frac{f_{i}^{2}}{r^{2}}r^{n-1}dr\geq(n+1)\int_{0}^{R}\frac{f_{i}^{2}}{r^{2}}r^{n-1}dr

since

\Delta u-\frac{n-1}{r^{2}}u+pU^{p-1}(y)u=0
has a positive kernel
-U_{r}
, and by
[
41
, Lemma 4.2]
, one has
Q_{1}(f_{i},f_{i})\geq 0
.
Specially, if
f_{j}=0
for
j=0,1,\dots,n
, we have (
7.21
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

The existence and uniqueness of the linear mapping
\phi^{\perp}=\phi^{\perp}[h^{\perp}]
are guaranteed by the classical parabolic theory. The form (
7.20
) is derived from the existence of every component
\phi_{j}
with

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{j}={\partial}_{rr}\phi_{j}+\frac{n-1}{r}{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}-\frac{\iota_{j}}{r^{2}}\phi_{j}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{j}+f_{1}\phi_{j}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}+h_{j}\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau)),\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j}(0,\tau)=\phi_{j}(R(\tau),\tau)=0\mbox{ \ for \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty),\quad\phi_{j}(r,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau_{0})).\end{cases}

By similar operation in mode
0
, we set
\phi^{\perp}=\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]+\tilde{\phi}[h^{\perp}]
, where
\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]
satisfies

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{*}=\Delta\phi_{*}+pU^{p-1}(1-\chi_{M})\phi_{*}+f_{1}\phi_{*}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{*}+h^{\perp}\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R},\\
\phi_{*}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathcal{D}_{R},\quad\phi_{*}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})},\end{cases}

and
\tilde{\phi}[h^{\perp}]
satisfies

(7.22)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\tilde{\phi}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}+pU^{p-1}\tilde{\phi}+f_{1}\tilde{\phi}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi}+pU^{p-1}\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R},\\
\tilde{\phi}=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathcal{D}_{R},\quad\tilde{\phi}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R(\tau_{0})}.\end{cases}

Under the assumption
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
, by Lemma
7.4
, we have

(7.23)

|\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]|\lesssim v\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}.

\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]
has the form

\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]=\sum\limits_{j=n+1}^{\infty}\phi_{*j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}

by the same reason as (
7.20
). By the same argument, there exists a linear mapping
\tilde{\phi}=\tilde{\phi}[h^{\perp}]
and
\tilde{\phi}
has the same form as (
7.20
). Thus we are able to apply (
7.21
) to
\tilde{\phi}
.

Multiplying (
7.22
) by
\tilde{\phi}
and integrating both sides, we have

\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}dy+Q(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\phi})=\int_{B_{R}}(f_{1}\tilde{\phi}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\phi})\tilde{\phi}dy+\int_{B_{R}}pU^{p-1}(y)\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]\tilde{\phi}dy.

By (
7.21
), (
7.2
) and Hölder inequality, one has

\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}dy+(n+\frac{1}{2})\int_{B_{R}}\frac{\tilde{\phi}^{2}}{|y|^{2}}dy\leq C\tau^{-d}\int_{B_{R}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}dy+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}}\left(pU^{p-1}(y)\chi_{M}\phi_{*}[h^{\perp}]|y|\right)^{2}dy.

Then, by (
7.23
) and the assumption
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
, we have

{\partial}_{\tau}\int_{B_{R}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}dy+R^{-2}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\phi}|^{2}dy\lesssim(v\theta_{Ra}^{0})^{2}\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}^{2}.

Since
\tilde{\phi}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0
and the assumption
R^{2}\ln R\ll\tau^{\min\{1,d\}}
, we have

\int_{B_{R}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}dy\lesssim e^{-\int^{\tau}R^{-2}(u)du}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}e^{\int^{s}R^{-2}(u)du}(v(s)\theta_{Ra}^{0}(s))^{2}ds\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}^{2}\lesssim(v\theta_{Ra}^{0}R\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a})^{2}.

Using the same argument in Lemma
7.5
, one has

(7.24)

|\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)|\lesssim v\theta_{Ra}^{0}R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}.

Combining (
7.23
), (
7.24
) and scaling argument, we get

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi^{\perp}|+|\phi^{\perp}|\lesssim v\left(\Theta_{Ra}^{0}(|y|)+\theta_{Ra}^{0}R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h^{\perp}\|_{v,a}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

The case for higher modes has been given in Lemma
7.7
. Since the fast spatial decay of the right hand side
h
cannot be recovered in non-orthogonal case in lower modes
i
,
0\leq i\leq n
, we transform the fast decay right hand side into slower decay function by solving the corresponding elliptic equation.

7.4.
Mode 0 with orthogonality

Consider

\Delta H^{0}+pU^{p-1}H^{0}=\tilde{h}^{0}(r,\tau)\mbox{\ \ in \ }{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}

where
\tilde{h}^{0}
is the extension of
h^{0}
as zero outside
{\mathcal{D}}_{R}
.
The orthogonal condition is reformulated as

\int_{0}^{R}h^{0}(r,\tau)Z_{n+1}(r)r^{n-1}dr=0\mbox{ \ for all \ }\tau>\tau_{0}.

Take
H^{0}(r,\tau)
as in the following form

H^{0}(r,\tau)=\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(r)\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{h}^{0}(s,\tau)Z_{n+1}(s)s^{n-1}ds-Z_{n+1}(r)\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{h}^{0}(s,\tau)\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(s)s^{n-1}ds,\mbox{ \ if \ }a\leq n-2,

H^{0}(r,\tau)=\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(r)\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{h}^{0}(s,\tau)Z_{n+1}(s)s^{n-1}ds+Z_{n+1}(r)\int_{r}^{\infty}\tilde{h}^{0}(s,\tau)\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(s)s^{n-1}ds,\mbox{ \ if \ }a>n-2,

where
\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(r)
is the other linearly independent kernel of the homogeneous equation, which satisfies that the Wronskian
W[Z_{n+1},\tilde{Z}_{n+1}]=r^{1-n}
,
\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(r)\sim r^{2-n}
if
r\rightarrow 0
and
\tilde{Z}_{n+1}(r)\sim 1
if
r\rightarrow\infty
.
It is straightforward to check

\|H^{0}\|_{v,\hat{a}_{0}}\lesssim\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a},

where
\hat{a}_{0}
is given in (
7.5
) and
a>0
is used to ensure that the spatial decay of
\tilde{h}^{0}(s,\tau)Z_{n+1}(s)s^{n-1}
is faster than
s^{-1}
for
s\geq 1
. Next,
consider

(7.25)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\Phi^{0}=\Delta\Phi^{0}+pU^{p-1}\Phi^{0}+H^{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{2R},\\
\Phi^{0}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=\bar{e}_{0}Z_{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{2R(\tau_{0})},\end{cases}

where
(\Phi^{0},\bar{e}_{0})
is given by Lemma
7.5
under the condition
f_{1}=f_{2}=0
. By scaling argument, one has

\langle y\rangle^{2}|\nabla^{2}\Phi^{0}|+\langle y\rangle|\nabla\Phi^{0}|+|\Phi^{0}|\lesssim v\left(\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{2-n}+\Theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}(|y|)\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a},

|\bar{e}_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})\theta_{R(\tau_{0})\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}.

Acting the operator
L:=\Delta+pU^{p-1}
on both sides of (
7.25
) and denoting
\phi_{1}^{0}=L\Phi^{0}
, we obtain

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{1}^{0}=\Delta\phi_{1}^{0}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{1}^{0}+h^{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathcal{D}_{R}\\
\phi_{1}^{0}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=\gamma_{0}\bar{e}_{0}Z_{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R}\end{cases}

with the following estimate

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi_{1}^{0}|+|\phi_{1}^{0}|\lesssim v\left(\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{-n}+\Theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}(|y|)\langle y\rangle^{-2}\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}.

Taking into account
f_{1}\phi^{0}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi^{0}
, we consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{2}^{0}=\Delta\phi_{2}^{0}+pU^{p-1}\phi_{2}^{0}+f_{1}\phi_{2}^{0}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{2}^{0}+f_{1}\phi_{1}^{0}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}^{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }{\mathcal{D}}_{R}\\
\phi_{2}^{0}(\cdot,\tau_{0})=e_{02}Z_{0}&\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{R}\end{cases}

where

\left|f_{1}\phi_{1}^{0}+f_{2}y\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}^{0}\right|\lesssim C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\langle y\rangle^{-2}\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}.

Using Lemma
7.5
again, one can find a solution
(\phi_{2}^{0},e_{02})
with the following estimates

|\phi_{2}^{0}|\lesssim C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\left(\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}+\ln R\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a},

|e_{02}|\lesssim C_{f}\tau_{0}^{-d}\min\{\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v(\tau_{0})\theta_{R(\tau_{0})\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\ln R(\tau_{0})\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}.

Finally, we take
(\phi^{0},e_{0})=(\phi_{1}^{0}+\phi_{2}^{0},\gamma_{0}\bar{e}_{0}+e_{02})
and conclude the result for mode
0
:

\displaystyle|\phi^{0}|\lesssim

\displaystyle v\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-n}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln R\langle y\rangle^{2-n}\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}

\displaystyle+v\left(\Theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}(|y|)\langle y\rangle^{-2}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}\min\{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{R\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\ln R\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a},

|e_{0}|\lesssim v(\tau_{0})\theta_{R(\tau_{0})\hat{a}_{0}}^{0}\left(1+C_{f}\tau_{0}^{-d}\min\{\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}},\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\lambda_{R(\tau_{0})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ln R(\tau_{0})\right)\|h^{0}\|_{v,2+a}.

7.5.
Modes
1
to
n
with orthogonality

Set
r=|y|
. Consider

h^{1}(y,\tau)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}
satisfying

\int_{B_{2R}}h^{1}Z_{j}=0

for all
j=1,\dots,n
,

\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)
. Then

(7.26)

\int_{0}^{2R}h_{j}(r,\tau)U_{r}(r)r^{n-1}dr=0\mbox{ \ for all \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)

where
U_{r}(r)=(n(n-2))^{\frac{n-2}{4}}(2-n)r(1+r^{2})^{-\frac{n}{2}}
.
Let
H=H_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}
satisfying
\mathcal{L}_{1}H_{j}+\tilde{h}_{j}=0
in
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}
,
where
\tilde{h}_{j}
is the extension of
h_{j}
as zero outside
{\mathcal{D}}_{R}
.
H_{j}
is given by

\displaystyle H_{j}(r,\tau)=

\displaystyle U_{r}(r)\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{\rho^{n-1}U_{r}(\rho)^{2}}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\tilde{h}_{j}(s,\tau)U_{r}(s)s^{n-1}dsd\rho\mbox{ \ for \ }-1<a\leq n-1,

\displaystyle H_{j}(r,\tau)=

\displaystyle-U_{r}(r)\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho^{n-1}U_{r}(\rho)^{2}}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\tilde{h}_{j}(s,\tau)U_{r}(s)s^{n-1}dsd\rho\mbox{ \ for \ }a>n-1

where
a>-1
is used to guarantee that the spatial decay of
\tilde{h}_{j}(s,\tau)U_{r}(s)s^{n-1}
is faster than
s^{-1}
. Using (
7.26
), one has
the following estimate

\|H_{j}\|_{v,\hat{a}_{1}}\lesssim\|h_{j}\|_{v,2+a},

where
\hat{a}_{1}
is given in (
7.5
).
Next, consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\Phi=\Delta\Phi+pU^{p-1}\Phi+H_{j}(r,\tau)\Upsilon_{j}\mbox{ \ in \ }D_{2R},\\
\Phi=0\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}D_{2R}\quad\Phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }B_{2R(\tau_{0})}.\end{cases}

By Lemma
7.6
, we find a solution
\Phi_{j}
with the estimate

|\Phi_{j}|\lesssim v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{1-n}\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a}.

It follows that

\phi_{j1}=L\Phi_{j}~~\mbox{ with }~~|\phi_{j1}|\lesssim v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{-1-n}\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a}.

Consider

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{j2}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\phi_{j2}+f_{1}\phi_{j2}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j2}+f_{1}\phi_{j1}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j1}\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau)),\ \tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j2}(0,\tau)=0=\phi_{j2}(R(\tau),\tau)\mbox{ \ for \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\infty),\quad\phi_{j2}(r,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ for \ }r\in(0,R(\tau_{0}))\end{cases}

where

|f_{1}\phi_{j1}+f_{2}r{\partial}_{r}\phi_{j1}|\lesssim C_{f}\tau^{-d}v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{-1-n}\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a}.

Using Lemma
7.6
again, we get
\phi_{j2}
with the following estimate

|\phi_{j2}|\lesssim C_{f}\tau^{-d}v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{2n}\langle y\rangle^{1-n}\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a},

Set
\phi_{j}[h_{j}]=\phi_{j1}+\phi_{j2}
. Then

\phi^{1}[h^{1}]=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\phi_{j}[h_{j}]\Upsilon_{j}

with the following estimate

\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi^{1}|+|\phi^{1}|\lesssim v\theta_{R\hat{a}_{1}}^{1}R^{n}\big(\langle y\rangle^{-1-n}+C_{f}\tau^{-d}R^{n}\langle y\rangle^{1-n}\big)\|h^{1}\|_{v,2+a}

as desired.

∎

Appendix A
Estimates for heat equations

Recalling
{\mathcal{T}}_{n}^{out}
defined in (
2.3
), we only require
t_{0}\geq 0
in Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
A.2
.

A.1.
Heat equation with right hand side
v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\}}

Lemma A.1
.

Assume
n>2
,
v(t)\geq 0
,
b\in{{\mathbb{R}}}
,
0\leq l_{1}(t)\leq l_{2}(t)\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
C_{l}^{-1}l_{i}(t)\leq l_{i}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{i}(t)
,
i=1,2
, for all
\frac{t}{2}\leq s\leq t
,
t\geq t_{0}\geq 0
, where
C_{*}>0,C_{l}\geq 1
.
Then

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{n}^{out}\left[v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\}}\right]\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{\frac{t_{0}}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}v(s)\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)})&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
l_{1}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ for \ }|x|\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }2<b<n\\
|x|^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{|x|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
|x|^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ for \ }l_{1}(t)<|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\\
|x|^{2-n}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ for \ }|x|>l_{2}(t)\end{cases}.

Proof.

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{n}^{out}\left[v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\}}\right]\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{\frac{t_{0}}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{l_{1}(s)\leq|y|\leq l_{2}(s)\}}dyds

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)\}}dyds:=u_{1}+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})u_{2}.

For
u_{1}
, notice
|y|\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
. For
|x|\leq 2C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

u_{1}\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{\frac{t_{0}}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{l_{1}(s)\leq|y|\leq l_{2}(s)\}}dyds\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{\frac{t_{0}}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}v(s)\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)})&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds.

For
|x|>2C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, one has
|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}
. Then

u_{1}\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{\frac{t_{0}}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}v(s)\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)})&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(s)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds.

Let us estimate
u_{2}
in different regions.

For
|x|\leq(2C_{l})^{-1}l_{1}(t)
,
since
\frac{|y|}{2}\leq|x-y|\leq 2|y|
, then

\displaystyle u_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)\}}dyds\sim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}\int_{\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{16C_{l}^{2}(t-s)}}^{\frac{C_{l}^{2}l_{2}^{2}(t)}{16(t-s)}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-1}dzds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-l_{2}^{2}(t)}+\int_{t-l_{2}^{2}(t)}^{t-l_{1}^{2}(t)}+\int_{t-l_{1}^{2}(t)}^{t}\cdots\right):=u_{21}+u_{22}+u_{23}\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
l_{1}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2.\end{cases}

In order to get the last inequality above, we need the following estimates.
For
u_{21}
, since
n>2
, we have

u_{21}\sim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-l_{2}^{2}(t)}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}\int_{\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{16C_{l}^{2}(t-s)}}^{\frac{C_{l}^{2}l_{2}^{2}(t)}{16(t-s)}}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-1}dzds\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle l_{2}^{2-n}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(t)l_{2}^{2-n}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n.\end{cases}

For
u_{22}
, since
l_{1}^{2}(t)\lesssim t-s\lesssim l_{2}^{2}(t)
, then

\displaystyle u_{22}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t-l_{2}^{2}(t)}^{t-l_{1}^{2}(t)}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{t-s}{l_{1}^{2}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
(\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{t-s})^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
l_{1}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2.\end{cases}

For
u_{23}
, we have

u_{23}\lesssim\int_{t-l_{1}^{2}(t)}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}\int_{\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{16C_{l}^{2}(t-s)}}^{\frac{C_{l}^{2}l_{2}^{2}(t)}{16(t-s)}}e^{-\frac{z}{2}}dzds\lesssim\int_{t-l_{1}^{2}(t)}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}e^{-\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{32C_{l}^{2}(t-s)}}ds\sim l_{1}^{2-b}(t).

For
(2C_{l})^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq 2C_{l}l_{2}(t)
, then

\displaystyle u_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\{(4C_{l})^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 2|x|\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{2|x|\leq|y|\leq 4C_{l}l_{2}(t)\}}\right)dyds

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u_{21}+u_{22}+u_{23}\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }2<b<n\\
|x|^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{|x|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
|x|^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n.\end{cases}

For the last inequality above, we need to estimate
u_{21}
,
u_{22}
and
u_{23}
. For
u_{21}
, since
n>2
, one has

u_{21}\leq\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{(4C_{l})^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}dyds\lesssim\begin{cases}|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
|x|^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{|x|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
|x|^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n.\end{cases}

For
u_{22}
, we have

u_{22}\lesssim|x|^{-b}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x-y|\leq 3|x|\}}dyds\sim|x|^{-b}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\frac{9|x|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n}{2}-1}dzds\sim|x|^{2-b}.

For
u_{23}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{23}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{2|x|\leq|y|\leq 4C_{l}l_{2}(t)\}}dyds\sim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\frac{|x|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}^{\frac{C_{l}^{2}l_{2}^{2}(t)}{t-s}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-1}dzds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{2C_{*}^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{2C_{*}^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8C_{l}^{2}C_{*}^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8C_{l}^{2}C_{*}^{2}}}^{t}\cdots\right):=u_{231}+u_{232}+u_{233}\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2.\end{cases}

In order to get the last inequality above, we need the following estimates. For
u_{231}
, we have

u_{231}\sim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{2C_{*}^{2}}}\int_{\frac{|x|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}^{\frac{C_{l}^{2}l_{2}^{2}(t)}{t-s}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-1}dzds\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
l_{2}^{2-n}(t)\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
|x|^{n-b}l_{2}^{2-n}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n.\end{cases}

For
u_{232}
, since
n>2
, we estimate

u_{232}\lesssim\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{2C_{*}^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8C_{l}^{2}C_{*}^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{|x|^{2}}{t-s})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
(\frac{|x|^{2}}{t-s})^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2.\end{cases}

For
u_{233}
, one has

u_{233}\lesssim\int_{t-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8C_{l}^{2}C_{*}^{2}}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8(t-s)}}ds\sim|x|^{2-b}\int_{C_{l}^{2}C_{*}^{2}}^{\infty}e^{-z}z^{\frac{b}{2}-2}dz\sim|x|^{2-b}.

For
|x|\geq 2C_{l}l_{2}(t)
, since
\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|x-y|\leq 2|x|
, then for
n>2
, it follows that

u_{2}\lesssim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}ds\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}\lesssim|x|^{2-n}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
l_{1}^{n-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n.\end{cases}

∎

A.2.
Heat equation with right hand side
v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\geq t^{1/2}\}}

Lemma A.2
.

Assume
n>0
,
v(t)\geq 0
,
b\in{{\mathbb{R}}}
,
t_{0}\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{n}^{out}\left[v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(ts^{-1})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds+t^{1-\frac{b}{2}}\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
|x|^{-b}\left(t\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})+\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)ds\right)+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}0&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(|x|s^{-\frac{1}{2}})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{cases}.

Proof.

By definition, we write

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{T}}_{n}^{out}\left[v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}\right]\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y|\geq s^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}dyds

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y|\geq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}dyds:=t^{-\frac{n}{2}}u_{1}+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t/2,t]}v(t_{1})u_{2}.

For
u_{1}
, when
|x|\leq 2t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}\lesssim\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\leq 4t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}dyds+\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16t}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{4t^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\}}dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(ts^{-1})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds+t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)ds\sim\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(ts^{-1})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds.

For
u_{1}
, when
|x|>2t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}=\

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 2|x|\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{2|x|\leq|y|\}}\right)dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim\

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}\left(e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{s^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}+|x|^{-b}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x-y|\leq 3|x|\}}+e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16t}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{2|x|\leq|y|\}}\right)dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim\

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}|x|^{n-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(|x|s^{-\frac{1}{2}})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds+t^{\frac{n}{2}}|x|^{-b}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)ds+t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8t}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)\begin{cases}0&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(|x|s^{-\frac{1}{2}})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
s^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}ds+t^{\frac{n}{2}}|x|^{-b}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}v(s)ds.

For
u_{2}
, when
|x|\leq 2^{-\frac{3}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have
|y|\geq 2|x|
. Then

u_{2}\leq\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y|\geq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}dyds\lesssim\int_{t/2}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}}e^{-\frac{t}{64(t-s)}}ds\sim t^{1-\frac{b}{2}}.

For
u_{2}
, when
|x|\geq 2^{-\frac{3}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, one has

\displaystyle u_{2}\leq\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\{9^{-1}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 4|x|\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{4|x|\leq|y|\}}\right)dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\left(e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{9^{-1}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}\}}+|x|^{-b}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x-y|\leq 5|x|\}}+e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16(t-s)}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{4|x|\leq|y|\}}\right)dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|x|^{2-n}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\begin{cases}|x|^{n-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle\ln(|x|t^{-\frac{1}{2}})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}+t|x|^{-b}+|x|^{2-b}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{2t}}\sim t|x|^{-b}.

∎

A.3.
Cauchy problem with initial value
\langle y\rangle^{-b}

Lemma A.3
.

For
n\geq 1
,
b\in{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
t>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle(4\pi t)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{b}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\leq\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}+|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{n}{2}}\ln(t+2)\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\leq\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}+\left(|x|^{-n}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\ln(|x|+2)\right)\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\leq\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}+\left(|x|^{-b}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}.

Proof.

Set

u(x,t)=(4\pi t)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\sim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\left(\int_{|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}+\int_{\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 2|x|}+\int_{2|x|\leq|y|}\right)e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy.

We estimate term by term:

\int_{|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\lesssim e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\int_{|y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\sim\begin{cases}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}|x|^{n}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq 1\\
\begin{cases}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}|x|^{n-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<n\\
e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\ln(|x|+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=n\\
e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>n\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>1\end{cases},

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{|x|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 2|x|}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-b}\int_{|x-y|\leq 3|x|}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}dy\sim\begin{cases}\langle x\rangle^{-b}|x|^{n}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\langle x\rangle^{-b}t^{\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{cases},

and

\int_{2|x|\leq|y|}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\leq\int_{2|x|\leq|y|}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy.

For
|x|\geq 1
, we have

\int_{2|x|\leq|y|}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\sim t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}\int_{\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}^{\infty}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-1}dz\lesssim\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b<n\\
\ln(\frac{t}{|x|^{2}})+1&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b=n\\
|x|^{n-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b>n\\
t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{cases}.

For
|x|<1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{2|x|\leq|y|}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{16t}}\langle y\rangle^{-b}dy\sim\int_{2|x|}^{2}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{16t}}r^{n-1}dr+\int_{2}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{16t}}r^{n-1-b}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{8t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\leq|x|^{2}\\
t^{\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|^{2}<t\leq 1\\
1&\mbox{ \ if \ }t>1\end{cases}+\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{8t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t<1\\
t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b<n\\
\ln(t+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b=n\\
1&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b>n\end{cases}\lesssim\begin{cases}t^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\leq|x|^{2}\\
t^{\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|^{2}<t\leq 1\\
t^{\frac{n-b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b<n\\
\ln(t+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b=n\\
1&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b>n\end{cases}.

Combining above estimates, one has

\displaystyle u(x,t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\leq 1\\
t^{-\frac{b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b<n\\
t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\ln(t+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b=n\\
t^{-\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }t\geq 1,b>n\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|<1\\
\begin{cases}t^{-\frac{b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b<n\\
t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\ln(t+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b=n\\
t^{-\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}},b>n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-b}\par&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}},b<n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-n}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\ln(|x|+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}},b=n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-b}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}},b>n\end{cases}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\geq 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{b}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b<n\\
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{n}{2}}\ln(t+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b=n\\
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|\leq\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b>n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-b}\par&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b<n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-n}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}\ln(|x|+2)&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b=n\\
\langle x\rangle^{-b}+t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{16t}}&\mbox{ \ if \ }|x|>\max\{1,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\},b>n.\end{cases}

This completes the proof of Lemma
A.3
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
3.1
: solving the outer problem

Proof.

It suffices to find a fixed point for
\psi=\mathcal{T}^{out}_{4}[\mathcal{G}[\psi,\phi,\mu_{1},\xi]]
. Set

w_{o}(x,t)=\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}+t|x|^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}\}}\right),

\|g\|_{o}=\sup\limits_{(x,t)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{4}\times(t_{0},\infty)}w^{-1}_{o}(x,t)|g(x,t)|,\quad B_{o}=\{g(x,t):\ \|g\|_{o}\leq D_{o}\},

where
D_{o}\geq 1
will be determined later.
For any
\psi_{1}\in B_{o}
,
let us estimate
\mathcal{G}[\psi_{1},\phi,\mu_{1},\xi]
term by term.
In this proof, we will apply Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
A.2
multiple times to estimate convolution
{\mathcal{T}}_{4}^{out}
and will not state them repetitively.

By the definitions of the norms (
3.8
), (
3.9
), (
3.10
), one has

|\phi(y,t)|+\langle y\rangle|\nabla\phi(y,t)|\lesssim(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|\phi\|_{i,\kappa-5\delta,a},

|\mu_{1}|+t|\mu_{1t}|\lesssim t\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\|\mu_{1}\|_{*1},\quad|\xi|+t|\xi_{t}|\lesssim t(\ln t)^{2}(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\|\xi\|_{*2}.

Then

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{x}\eta_{R}\mu^{-1}\phi(\frac{x-\xi}{\mu},t)\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}}\mu^{-1}(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}\langle y\rangle^{-a}\|\phi\|_{i,\kappa-5\delta,a}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}},

\displaystyle\left|2\nabla_{x}\eta_{R}\cdot\mu^{-2}\nabla_{y}\phi(\frac{x-\xi}{\mu},t)\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle(\mu_{0}R)^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}}\mu^{-2}(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}\langle y\rangle^{-1-a}\|\phi\|_{i,\kappa-5\delta,a}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}},

\displaystyle\left|{\partial}_{t}\eta_{R}\mu^{-1}\phi(\frac{x-\xi}{\mu},t)\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\eta(\frac{x-\xi}{\mu_{0}R})\cdot(\frac{-\xi_{t}}{\mu_{0}R}-\frac{x-\xi}{\mu_{0}R}\frac{(\mu_{0}R)_{t}}{\mu_{0}R})\mu^{-1}\phi(\frac{x-\xi}{\mu},t)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{2}(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}},

where we have used
\gamma<\frac{1}{2}
and
5\delta-\kappa<-1
in the last inequality. Then one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{out}_{4}\left[(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R\leq|x-\xi|\leq 2\mu_{0}R\}}\right]\lesssim\mathcal{T}^{out}_{4}\left[(\mu_{0}R)^{-2}\ln t(t(\ln t)^{2})^{5\delta-\kappa}R^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{0}R/2\leq|x|\leq 4\mu_{0}R\}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

large depending on

large enough, we have

.
